Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `dsp48e2_macc` (precision INT27)
- what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
- computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: `OPMODE` chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
- input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
- output ports: P[48]
- arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on `A` and 17 bits on `B`; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `CLK`, the block has exactly these registers. `A2` loads A at a clock edge where `CEA2` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTA`. `B2` loads B at a clock edge where `CEB2` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTB`. `P` loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where `CEP` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTP`; the output `P` is this register. `P` is always registered; `C` and `OPMODE` reach `P` without an input register.
- timing: `A` and `B` reach `P` 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in `P` after edge k+1); `C` and `OPMODE` have no register before the one that drives `P`: the value present at a clock edge is in `P` after that edge (1 cycle).
- control `OPMODE`: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:
  - 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
  - 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
  - 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
  - 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
  - 110000000 (load C): P <= C
  - 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
  - 010000000 (hold): P <= P
  - 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
  - any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
- resets and clock enables (resets `RSTA`, `RSTB` and `RSTP`; clock enables `CEA2`, `CEB2` and `CEP`): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
- power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so `P` is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; `OPMODE` may be a constant legal value.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, conv_layer.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified conv_layer, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 84 on 84 DSP tiles, all in two_mult_18x19 mode (1 to a tile). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

idsource lineleft operandright operandinstances
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;i_multiplicand, 16 bits, unsignedi_multiplier, 16 bits, unsigned84
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

No rewrite
  • REGIONS: NONE: You propose no rewrite. The plan states why, from what you were given. The run then ends and keeps the original design.
What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the REGIONS line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: conv_layer.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's add_out_reg accumulator (the qadd sum fed back through add_in1/add_in2), one per seq_mac instance; no reduction is shared between instances
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance (84 instances) — CLK=clk; A={11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned zero-extended to 27, top bit 0); B={2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned zero-extended to 18, top bit 0); C={31'b0, (a_flopped + b_flopped)} (17-bit unsigned sum of the two flopped operands, zero-extended to 48); OPMODE=(eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 (load C) : 9'b000100101 (multiply-accumulate); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=reset; CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; output P[31:0] replaces the register add_out_reg and drives the existing down-cast logic that feeds out (P[47:32] unused)
PLAN:

Candidate regions

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (line 2854, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances): converted. It is the only candidate; nothing is left unconverted.

What the block absorbs

Per seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the block takes over three things at once: the qmult product, the product pipeline register mul_out_reg, and the accumulator add_out_reg together with the qadd adder (conv_layer.v:2859-2866) and its add_in1/add_in2 muxes. The DSP count does not change (84 before, 84 after; 84 ≤ 306 tiles), but ~64 soft flip-flops, a 32-bit soft adder and two 32-bit muxes per instance are removed, at the cost of one 17-bit soft adder per instance on the C path.

Readers checked: mul_out is read only by mul_out_reg; mul_out_reg only by add_in2; add_out/add_out_reg only by the down-cast at conv_layer.v:2823-2835 (and by add_in1); a_flopped/b_flopped are read by the multiply and by add_in1/add_in2, so they stay in soft logic. out is the only module output. qmult and qadd are instantiated nowhere else (only conv_layer.v:2793 and 2810).

Edits to make, inside module seq_mac only

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (conv_layer.v:2783-2791) exactly as it is.
  2. Delete the qmult mult_u1 instance (2793), the mul_out/mul_out_reg declarations and the mul_out_reg always block (2795-2801).
  3. Delete add_in1, add_in2, add_out, the qadd add_u1 instance and the add_out_reg always block (2804-2818). Re-declare add_out_reg as wire [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg; and assign add_out_reg = P_dsp[31:0]; so the down-cast at 2823-2835 and the out` always block (2838-2844) are untouched.
  4. Add wire [\DWIDTH:0] eltsum = a_flopped + b_flopped; (17 bits, no truncation possible) and wire [8:0] dsp_opmode = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101;`.
  5. Instantiate, with wire [47:0] P_dsp;:
    dsp48e2_macc u_dsp (.CLK(clk), .A({11'b0, a_flopped}), .B({2'b0, b_flopped}), .C({31'b0, eltsum}), .OPMODE(dsp_opmode), .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset), .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1), .P(P_dsp));
  6. Do not add a module body for dsp48e2_macc to conv_layer.v (the behavioural model is supplied separately); leave qmult/qadd module definitions in the file — they become uninstantiated and are swept.
  7. Change nothing outside seq_mac; processing_element and everything above it are untouched.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle identical (edge k samples the values present before it)

  • Original: a_flopped(k) = reset ? 0 : a(k); mul_out_reg(k) = reset ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1)*b_flopped(k-1); add_out_reg(k) = reset ? 0 : ( M ? a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) : add_out_reg(k-1)+mul_out_reg(k-1) ), with M = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add.
  • After: A2(k) = reset ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1), B2(k) = reset ? 0 : b_flopped(k-1) (CEA2/CEB2 tied high, RSTA/RSTB = reset), so A2(k)*B2(k) equals mul_out_reg(k) on every cycle including the reset cycle, because the original zeroes the product register exactly when both operand registers are zeroed. P(k) = reset ? 0 (RSTP wins over CEP) : ( M ? C(k) : P(k-1)+A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) ), and C(k) = a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) because C and OPMODE are unregistered and act at the edge that loads P. By induction P[31:0] = add_out_reg: both operands are zero-extended unsigned 16-bit values, so the signed 27x18 product is exact, non-negative and its low 32 bits are the unsigned 32-bit product; the 48-bit accumulate taken mod 2^32 is the original 32-bit wrap-around add, and the upper 16 bits of P are never read. The eltwise sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values needs 17 bits, so it is exact in both designs.
  • Resets: every design reset of seq_mac (mac_reset = reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) at conv_layer.v:2647) drives RSTA, RSTB and RSTP together, which reproduces the original's simultaneous clearing of a_flopped/b_flopped (kept soft), mul_out_reg and add_out_reg, on mid-run resets as well as at start-up. The block's registers power up at 0, matching the original's registers starting at 0; no soft register is added, so there is no undefined start-up value to rely on and no X can reach out. OPMODE only ever takes the two listed legal encodings.

Register stages, operands to result

  • Before (MAC mode): a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). Accumulation happens at stage 3.
  • After (MAC mode): a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → block A2/B2 (2) → block P, accumulating (3) → soft out (4). Same four stages, same latency, same accumulation cycle.
  • Before (eltwise-add mode): a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3).
  • After (eltwise-add mode): a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → 17-bit soft adder → block C/P load (2) → soft out (3). Same three stages.

No assumptions are needed: the rewrite matches the original on arbitrary a, b, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add and reset sequences, so there is no ASSUMES: line.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the one path the block cannot swallow is the eltwise-add sum, which must reach C combinationally from a_flopped/b_flopped and so puts a 17-bit soft adder in series with the block's 2.140 ns C-to-P delay. If seq_mac registered a + b one stage earlier (a flop loading a + b beside a_flopped/b_flopped, with add_in1/add_in2 reading it), C would be driven straight from a flip-flop and the stage would be the block's 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 ns alone.

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v"

output
1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is conv_layer.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

Multiplies in the unmodified conv_layer, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 84 on 84 DSP tiles, all in two_mult_18x19 mode (1 to a tile). The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

idsource lineleft operandright operandinstances
qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;i_multiplicand, 16 bits, unsignedi_multiplier, 16 bits, unsigned84
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's add_out_reg accumulator (the qadd sum fed back through add_in1/add_in2), one per seq_mac instance; no reduction is shared between instances
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance (84 instances) — CLK=clk; A={11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned zero-extended to 27, top bit 0); B={2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned zero-extended to 18, top bit 0); C={31'b0, (a_flopped + b_flopped)} (17-bit unsigned sum of the two flopped operands, zero-extended to 48); OPMODE=(eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 (load C) : 9'b000100101 (multiply-accumulate); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=reset; CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; output P[31:0] replaces the register add_out_reg and drives the existing down-cast logic that feeds out (P[47:32] unused)
PLAN:

Candidate regions

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (line 2854, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances): converted. It is the only candidate; nothing is left unconverted.

What the block absorbs

Per seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the block takes over three things at once: the qmult product, the product pipeline register mul_out_reg, and the accumulator add_out_reg together with the qadd adder (conv_layer.v:2859-2866) and its add_in1/add_in2 muxes. The DSP count does not change (84 before, 84 after; 84 ≤ 306 tiles), but ~64 soft flip-flops, a 32-bit soft adder and two 32-bit muxes per instance are removed, at the cost of one 17-bit soft adder per instance on the C path.

Readers checked: mul_out is read only by mul_out_reg; mul_out_reg only by add_in2; add_out/add_out_reg only by the down-cast at conv_layer.v:2823-2835 (and by add_in1); a_flopped/b_flopped are read by the multiply and by add_in1/add_in2, so they stay in soft logic. out is the only module output. qmult and qadd are instantiated nowhere else (only conv_layer.v:2793 and 2810).

Edits to make, inside module seq_mac only

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (conv_layer.v:2783-2791) exactly as it is.
  2. Delete the qmult mult_u1 instance (2793), the mul_out/mul_out_reg declarations and the mul_out_reg always block (2795-2801).
  3. Delete add_in1, add_in2, add_out, the qadd add_u1 instance and the add_out_reg always block (2804-2818). Re-declare add_out_reg as wire [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg; and assign add_out_reg = P_dsp[31:0]; so the down-cast at 2823-2835 and the out` always block (2838-2844) are untouched.
  4. Add wire [\DWIDTH:0] eltsum = a_flopped + b_flopped; (17 bits, no truncation possible) and wire [8:0] dsp_opmode = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101;`.
  5. Instantiate, with wire [47:0] P_dsp;:
    dsp48e2_macc u_dsp (.CLK(clk), .A({11'b0, a_flopped}), .B({2'b0, b_flopped}), .C({31'b0, eltsum}), .OPMODE(dsp_opmode), .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset), .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1), .P(P_dsp));
  6. Do not add a module body for dsp48e2_macc to conv_layer.v (the behavioural model is supplied separately); leave qmult/qadd module definitions in the file — they become uninstantiated and are swept.
  7. Change nothing outside seq_mac; processing_element and everything above it are untouched.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle identical (edge k samples the values present before it)

  • Original: a_flopped(k) = reset ? 0 : a(k); mul_out_reg(k) = reset ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1)*b_flopped(k-1); add_out_reg(k) = reset ? 0 : ( M ? a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) : add_out_reg(k-1)+mul_out_reg(k-1) ), with M = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add.
  • After: A2(k) = reset ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1), B2(k) = reset ? 0 : b_flopped(k-1) (CEA2/CEB2 tied high, RSTA/RSTB = reset), so A2(k)*B2(k) equals mul_out_reg(k) on every cycle including the reset cycle, because the original zeroes the product register exactly when both operand registers are zeroed. P(k) = reset ? 0 (RSTP wins over CEP) : ( M ? C(k) : P(k-1)+A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) ), and C(k) = a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) because C and OPMODE are unregistered and act at the edge that loads P. By induction P[31:0] = add_out_reg: both operands are zero-extended unsigned 16-bit values, so the signed 27x18 product is exact, non-negative and its low 32 bits are the unsigned 32-bit product; the 48-bit accumulate taken mod 2^32 is the original 32-bit wrap-around add, and the upper 16 bits of P are never read. The eltwise sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values needs 17 bits, so it is exact in both designs.
  • Resets: every design reset of seq_mac (mac_reset = reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) at conv_layer.v:2647) drives RSTA, RSTB and RSTP together, which reproduces the original's simultaneous clearing of a_flopped/b_flopped (kept soft), mul_out_reg and add_out_reg, on mid-run resets as well as at start-up. The block's registers power up at 0, matching the original's registers starting at 0; no soft register is added, so there is no undefined start-up value to rely on and no X can reach out. OPMODE only ever takes the two listed legal encodings.

Register stages, operands to result

  • Before (MAC mode): a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). Accumulation happens at stage 3.
  • After (MAC mode): a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → block A2/B2 (2) → block P, accumulating (3) → soft out (4). Same four stages, same latency, same accumulation cycle.
  • Before (eltwise-add mode): a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3).
  • After (eltwise-add mode): a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → 17-bit soft adder → block C/P load (2) → soft out (3). Same three stages.

No assumptions are needed: the rewrite matches the original on arbitrary a, b, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add and reset sequences, so there is no ASSUMES: line.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the one path the block cannot swallow is the eltwise-add sum, which must reach C combinationally from a_flopped/b_flopped and so puts a 17-bit soft adder in series with the block's 2.140 ns C-to-P delay. If seq_mac registered a + b one stage earlier (a flop loading a + b beside a_flopped/b_flopped, with add_in1/add_in2 reading it), C would be driven straight from a flip-flop and the stage would be the block's 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 ns alone.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/.

2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are

28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiply in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (module qmult).
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the approved plan calls for.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's add_out_reg accumulator (the qadd sum fed back through add_in1/add_in2), one per seq_mac instance; no reduction is shared between instances
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance (84 instances) — CLK=clk; A={11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned zero-extended to 27, top bit 0); B={2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned zero-extended to 18, top bit 0); C={31'b0, (a_flopped + b_flopped)} (17-bit unsigned sum of the two flopped operands, zero-extended to 48); OPMODE=(eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 (load C) : 9'b000100101 (multiply-accumulate); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=reset; CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; output P[31:0] replaces the register add_out_reg and drives the existing down-cast logic that feeds out (P[47:32] unused)
PLAN:

Candidate regions

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (line 2854, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances): converted. It is the only candidate; nothing is left unconverted.

What the block absorbs

Per seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the block takes over three things at once: the qmult product, the product pipeline register mul_out_reg, and the accumulator add_out_reg together with the qadd adder (conv_layer.v:2859-2866) and its add_in1/add_in2 muxes. The DSP count does not change (84 before, 84 after; 84 ≤ 306 tiles), but ~64 soft flip-flops, a 32-bit soft adder and two 32-bit muxes per instance are removed, at the cost of one 17-bit soft adder per instance on the C path.

Readers checked: mul_out is read only by mul_out_reg; mul_out_reg only by add_in2; add_out/add_out_reg only by the down-cast at conv_layer.v:2823-2835 (and by add_in1); a_flopped/b_flopped are read by the multiply and by add_in1/add_in2, so they stay in soft logic. out is the only module output. qmult and qadd are instantiated nowhere else (only conv_layer.v:2793 and 2810).

Edits to make, inside module seq_mac only

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (conv_layer.v:2783-2791) exactly as it is.
  2. Delete the qmult mult_u1 instance (2793), the mul_out/mul_out_reg declarations and the mul_out_reg always block (2795-2801).
  3. Delete add_in1, add_in2, add_out, the qadd add_u1 instance and the add_out_reg always block (2804-2818). Re-declare add_out_reg as wire [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg; and assign add_out_reg = P_dsp[31:0]; so the down-cast at 2823-2835 and the out` always block (2838-2844) are untouched.
  4. Add wire [\DWIDTH:0] eltsum = a_flopped + b_flopped; (17 bits, no truncation possible) and wire [8:0] dsp_opmode = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101;`.
  5. Instantiate, with wire [47:0] P_dsp;:
    dsp48e2_macc u_dsp (.CLK(clk), .A({11'b0, a_flopped}), .B({2'b0, b_flopped}), .C({31'b0, eltsum}), .OPMODE(dsp_opmode), .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset), .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1), .P(P_dsp));
  6. Do not add a module body for dsp48e2_macc to conv_layer.v (the behavioural model is supplied separately); leave qmult/qadd module definitions in the file — they become uninstantiated and are swept.
  7. Change nothing outside seq_mac; processing_element and everything above it are untouched.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle identical (edge k samples the values present before it)

  • Original: a_flopped(k) = reset ? 0 : a(k); mul_out_reg(k) = reset ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1)*b_flopped(k-1); add_out_reg(k) = reset ? 0 : ( M ? a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) : add_out_reg(k-1)+mul_out_reg(k-1) ), with M = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add.
  • After: A2(k) = reset ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1), B2(k) = reset ? 0 : b_flopped(k-1) (CEA2/CEB2 tied high, RSTA/RSTB = reset), so A2(k)*B2(k) equals mul_out_reg(k) on every cycle including the reset cycle, because the original zeroes the product register exactly when both operand registers are zeroed. P(k) = reset ? 0 (RSTP wins over CEP) : ( M ? C(k) : P(k-1)+A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) ), and C(k) = a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) because C and OPMODE are unregistered and act at the edge that loads P. By induction P[31:0] = add_out_reg: both operands are zero-extended unsigned 16-bit values, so the signed 27x18 product is exact, non-negative and its low 32 bits are the unsigned 32-bit product; the 48-bit accumulate taken mod 2^32 is the original 32-bit wrap-around add, and the upper 16 bits of P are never read. The eltwise sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values needs 17 bits, so it is exact in both designs.
  • Resets: every design reset of seq_mac (mac_reset = reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) at conv_layer.v:2647) drives RSTA, RSTB and RSTP together, which reproduces the original's simultaneous clearing of a_flopped/b_flopped (kept soft), mul_out_reg and add_out_reg, on mid-run resets as well as at start-up. The block's registers power up at 0, matching the original's registers starting at 0; no soft register is added, so there is no undefined start-up value to rely on and no X can reach out. OPMODE only ever takes the two listed legal encodings.

Register stages, operands to result

  • Before (MAC mode): a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). Accumulation happens at stage 3.
  • After (MAC mode): a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → block A2/B2 (2) → block P, accumulating (3) → soft out (4). Same four stages, same latency, same accumulation cycle.
  • Before (eltwise-add mode): a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3).
  • After (eltwise-add mode): a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → 17-bit soft adder → block C/P load (2) → soft out (3). Same three stages.

No assumptions are needed: the rewrite matches the original on arbitrary a, b, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add and reset sequences, so there is no ASSUMES: line.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
2	# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
3	# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
4	# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
5	# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
6	# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
7	# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
8	# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
9	# of the target block's name fails this check.
10	# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
11	# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
12	# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
13	# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
14	set -u
15	f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
16	here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
17	lib=""
18	[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
19	[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
20	target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
21	if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
22	  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
23	  exit 1
24	fi
25	out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
26	rc=$?
27	if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
28	  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
29	  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
30	  echo "$out" | tail -10
31	  exit 1
32	fi
33	echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"

1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.

  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's add_out_reg accumulator (the qadd sum fed back through add_in1/add_in2), one per seq_mac instance; no reduction is shared between instances
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance (84 instances) — CLK=clk; A={11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned zero-extended to 27, top bit 0); B={2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned zero-extended to 18, top bit 0); C={31'b0, (a_flopped + b_flopped)} (17-bit unsigned sum of the two flopped operands, zero-extended to 48); OPMODE=(eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 (load C) : 9'b000100101 (multiply-accumulate); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=reset; CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; output P[31:0] replaces the register add_out_reg and drives the existing down-cast logic that feeds out (P[47:32] unused)
PLAN:

Candidate regions

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (line 2854, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16x16 unsigned, 84 instances): converted. It is the only candidate; nothing is left unconverted.

What the block absorbs

Per seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846) the block takes over three things at once: the qmult product, the product pipeline register mul_out_reg, and the accumulator add_out_reg together with the qadd adder (conv_layer.v:2859-2866) and its add_in1/add_in2 muxes. The DSP count does not change (84 before, 84 after; 84 ≤ 306 tiles), but ~64 soft flip-flops, a 32-bit soft adder and two 32-bit muxes per instance are removed, at the cost of one 17-bit soft adder per instance on the C path.

Readers checked: mul_out is read only by mul_out_reg; mul_out_reg only by add_in2; add_out/add_out_reg only by the down-cast at conv_layer.v:2823-2835 (and by add_in1); a_flopped/b_flopped are read by the multiply and by add_in1/add_in2, so they stay in soft logic. out is the only module output. qmult and qadd are instantiated nowhere else (only conv_layer.v:2793 and 2810).

Edits to make, inside module seq_mac only

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (conv_layer.v:2783-2791) exactly as it is.
  2. Delete the qmult mult_u1 instance (2793), the mul_out/mul_out_reg declarations and the mul_out_reg always block (2795-2801).
  3. Delete add_in1, add_in2, add_out, the qadd add_u1 instance and the add_out_reg always block (2804-2818). Re-declare add_out_reg as wire [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg; and assign add_out_reg = P_dsp[31:0]; so the down-cast at 2823-2835 and the out` always block (2838-2844) are untouched.
  4. Add wire [\DWIDTH:0] eltsum = a_flopped + b_flopped; (17 bits, no truncation possible) and wire [8:0] dsp_opmode = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101;`.
  5. Instantiate, with wire [47:0] P_dsp;:
    dsp48e2_macc u_dsp (.CLK(clk), .A({11'b0, a_flopped}), .B({2'b0, b_flopped}), .C({31'b0, eltsum}), .OPMODE(dsp_opmode), .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset), .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1), .P(P_dsp));
  6. Do not add a module body for dsp48e2_macc to conv_layer.v (the behavioural model is supplied separately); leave qmult/qadd module definitions in the file — they become uninstantiated and are swept.
  7. Change nothing outside seq_mac; processing_element and everything above it are untouched.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle identical (edge k samples the values present before it)

  • Original: a_flopped(k) = reset ? 0 : a(k); mul_out_reg(k) = reset ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1)*b_flopped(k-1); add_out_reg(k) = reset ? 0 : ( M ? a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) : add_out_reg(k-1)+mul_out_reg(k-1) ), with M = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add.
  • After: A2(k) = reset ? 0 : a_flopped(k-1), B2(k) = reset ? 0 : b_flopped(k-1) (CEA2/CEB2 tied high, RSTA/RSTB = reset), so A2(k)*B2(k) equals mul_out_reg(k) on every cycle including the reset cycle, because the original zeroes the product register exactly when both operand registers are zeroed. P(k) = reset ? 0 (RSTP wins over CEP) : ( M ? C(k) : P(k-1)+A2(k-1)*B2(k-1) ), and C(k) = a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) because C and OPMODE are unregistered and act at the edge that loads P. By induction P[31:0] = add_out_reg: both operands are zero-extended unsigned 16-bit values, so the signed 27x18 product is exact, non-negative and its low 32 bits are the unsigned 32-bit product; the 48-bit accumulate taken mod 2^32 is the original 32-bit wrap-around add, and the upper 16 bits of P are never read. The eltwise sum of two zero-extended 16-bit values needs 17 bits, so it is exact in both designs.
  • Resets: every design reset of seq_mac (mac_reset = reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) at conv_layer.v:2647) drives RSTA, RSTB and RSTP together, which reproduces the original's simultaneous clearing of a_flopped/b_flopped (kept soft), mul_out_reg and add_out_reg, on mid-run resets as well as at start-up. The block's registers power up at 0, matching the original's registers starting at 0; no soft register is added, so there is no undefined start-up value to rely on and no X can reach out. OPMODE only ever takes the two listed legal encodings.

Register stages, operands to result

  • Before (MAC mode): a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg (3) → out (4). Accumulation happens at stage 3.
  • After (MAC mode): a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → block A2/B2 (2) → block P, accumulating (3) → soft out (4). Same four stages, same latency, same accumulation cycle.
  • Before (eltwise-add mode): a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → add_out_reg (2) → out (3).
  • After (eltwise-add mode): a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → 17-bit soft adder → block C/P load (2) → soft out (3). Same three stages.

No assumptions are needed: the rewrite matches the original on arbitrary a, b, eltwise_mode, eltwise_add and reset sequences, so there is no ASSUMES: line.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the one path the block cannot swallow is the eltwise-add sum, which must reach C combinationally from a_flopped/b_flopped and so puts a 17-bit soft adder in series with the block's 2.140 ns C-to-P delay. If seq_mac registered a + b one stage earlier (a flop loading a + b beside a_flopped/b_flopped, with add_in1/add_in2 reading it), C would be driven straight from a flip-flop and the stage would be the block's 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 ns alone.

The edit

simulation passed: 1156 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Simulation shows that every output of seq_mac matches on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles); it does not show that the edit follows the plan or the correctness rules. That is your check.

The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the source and the simulation artifacts.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance — the edit is confined to module seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2839), exactly the edits items 1-7 name: a_flopped/b_flopped block untouched (2779-2787); qmult mult_u1, mul_out, mul_out_reg and its always block deleted; add_in1/add_in2/add_out/qadd add_u1/add_out_reg always block deleted and add_out_reg re-declared as a wire fed from P_dsp[31:0] (2810-2811), leaving the down-cast (2816-2828) and the out register (2831-2837) byte-identical; eltsum and dsp_opmode added (2791-2792); no dsp48e2_macc body added (only the instance at 2795); qmult/qadd definitions left in place and now uninstantiated (sole call sites were in seq_mac). The run log's subtree check confirms nothing outside qadd/qmult/seq_mac changed. 7 matmul_4x4_systolic instances × 12 live PEs = 84 blocks ≤ 306 tiles, as the plan states.

Ports — all eleven inputs connected by name: CLK=clk, A={11'b0,a_flopped} (27 b, bit 26 = 0), B={2'b0,b_flopped} (18 b, bit 17 = 0), C={31'b0,eltsum} (48 b), OPMODE=dsp_opmode, RSTA/RSTB/RSTP=reset, CEA2/CEB2/CEP=1'b1. P drives add_out_reg → the existing down-cast → out, as planned; P[47:32] unused.

Correctness

  • Terms/visibility: mul_out, mul_out_reg, add_out had no readers outside the deleted logic; a_flopped/b_flopped are read by the eltwise path, so they stay in soft logic and the sum is recomputed outside the block at C. out is the only module output.
  • Arithmetic: 16-bit unsigned operands zero-extended, both ≤ the 26/17-bit unsigned limits and with zero top bits, so the signed 27×18 product equals qmult's unsigned product exactly; the 48-bit accumulate truncated to 32 bits is congruent to the original 32-bit wrapping add; eltsum is evaluated in 17-bit context, so no truncation.
  • Registers/reset: A2/B2 reproduce mul_out_reg cycle-for-cycle including reset cycles (the original clears product and operand registers under the same reset); P under MACC/load-C reproduces add_out_reg, with C and OPMODE unregistered and acting at the loading edge, matching the original's combinational add_in1/add_in2 muxes. RSTP wins over CEP, matching the synchronous clear. eltwise_mul/eltwise_sub (eltwise_mode=1, eltwise_add=0) correctly take the MACC encoding, as in the original muxes. Only the two legal OPMODE encodings are ever driven. No new soft registers, so no reliance on unknown power-up state; block registers power up at 0 as the original's do.
  • Assumptions: none needed, and none claimed.
