2807:assign add_in1 = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? {{`DWIDTH{1'b0}}, a_flopped} : add_out_reg;
2808:assign add_in2 = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? {{`DWIDTH{1'b0}}, b_flopped} : mul_out_reg;
2810:qadd add_u1(.a(add_in1), .b(add_in2), .c(add_out));
2814:    add_out_reg <= 0;
2816:    add_out_reg <= add_out;
2824:    (add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] == 0) ?  //positive number
2826:           (|(add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 1, that means overlfow
2827:             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : //sign bit and then all 1s
2828:             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , add_out_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]} 
2832:           (|(add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 0, that means overlfow
2833:             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , add_out_reg[`DWIDTH-2:0]} :
2834:             {add_out_reg[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}} //sign bit and then all 0s
2849:module qmult(i_multiplicand,i_multiplier,o_result);
2859:module qadd(a,b,c);
Call 7 · editor · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 18 · out 4902 · reasoning 548 · cache read 184696 · cache write 19906 · 59.0 s
prompt
Task

Edit conv_layer.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiply in the approved plan: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (module qmult).
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the approved plan calls for.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh conv_layer.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator (the original add_out_reg / qadd feedback), one per seq_mac instance; no reduction is shared between instances
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance (84 instances) — CLK=clk; A={11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, top bit 0); B={2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, top bit 0); C={31'b0, eltsum_reg} (new 17-bit soft register holding a+b registered one stage earlier, zero-extended to 48); OPMODE=(eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 (load C) : 9'b000100101 (multiply-accumulate); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=reset (the module's reset, i.e. mac_reset); CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; output P[31:0] replaces the register add_out_reg and drives the existing down-cast logic that feeds out (P[47:32] unused)
PLAN:

This revision in one line

The kept candidate is correct and already −20.2%; its critical path is now entirely the eltwise-add sum: a 16+16→17-bit soft adder sitting combinationally between a_flopped/b_flopped and the block's unregistered C pin, whose path runs on through the block's 2.140 ns to the P register (measured at seed 1: 0.060 clk-to-Q + 1.939 wire + 0.344 carry chain + 1.001 wire + 2.140 = 5.523 ns arrival). This revision moves that adder one register stage earlier — it computes a + b from the module inputs into a new 17-bit flop eltsum_reg — so C is driven straight from a flip-flop and the C→P stage becomes 0.060 + routing + 2.159 ns, with the adder in its own short flop-to-flop stage. Everything else is the kept candidate unchanged.

Candidate regions

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (conv_layer.v:2854, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16×16 unsigned, 84 instances): converted, on 84 blocks. It is the only candidate; nothing is left unconverted. qmult is instantiated only at conv_layer.v:2793 and qadd only at conv_layer.v:2810, both inside seq_mac.

What each block absorbs (unchanged from the kept candidate)

Per seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): the qmult product, the product pipeline register mul_out_reg, the accumulator register add_out_reg with its qadd adder and the add_in1/add_in2 muxes. DSP tiles stay at 84 (≤ 306).

Readers re-checked in the source: mul_out is read only by mul_out_reg (2799); mul_out_reg only by add_in2 (2808); add_out only by add_out_reg (2816); add_out_reg by add_in1 (2807) and by the down-cast at 2823-2835; a_flopped/b_flopped by the multiply (2793) and by add_in1/add_in2 (2807-2808); out (2774, 2838-2844) is the module's only output. Nothing inside the block is read elsewhere.

Edits to make, inside module seq_mac only (against the unmodified conv_layer.v in this directory)

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (2783-2791) exactly as it is; a_flopped/b_flopped now feed only A/B.
  2. Delete wire [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] add_out; (2775), wire [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out; reg [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_reg; (2780-2781), the qmult mult_u1 instance (2793) and the mul_out_reg` always block (2795-2801).
  3. Delete add_in1, add_in2, the add_out_reg declaration, the two assigns, the qadd add_u1 instance and the add_out_reg always block (2804-2818).

  4. Add the new operand-sum register, reset by the module's reset exactly like a_flopped/b_flopped:

    verilog
      reg [`DWIDTH:0] eltsum_reg;            // = a_flopped + b_flopped, one stage earlier
  always @(posedge clk) begin
    if (reset) eltsum_reg <= 0;
    else       eltsum_reg <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
  end

    (17 bits, so the unsigned 16+16 sum never truncates; the explicit {1'b0,…} keeps the width unambiguous.)

  5. Add the control and the block:

    verilog
      wire [8:0]  dsp_opmode = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101;
  wire [47:0] P_dsp;
  dsp48e2_macc u_dsp (
    .CLK(clk), .A({11'b0, a_flopped}), .B({2'b0, b_flopped}),
    .C({31'b0, eltsum_reg}), .OPMODE(dsp_opmode),
    .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset),
    .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1), .P(P_dsp));
  wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
  assign add_out_reg = P_dsp[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];
  6. Leave the down-cast (2820-2835) and the out always block (2838-2844) untouched; leave the qmult/qadd module definitions in the file (they become uninstantiated and are swept); do not add a body for dsp48e2_macc (the model is supplied in sim_reference/).
  7. Change nothing outside seq_mac.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle identical (edge k samples the values present before it; R(k) = reset at edge k, M(k) = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add at edge k)

  • Original: a_flopped(k) = R(k)?0:a(k), likewise b_flopped; mul_out_reg(k) = R(k)?0:a_flopped(k-1)·b_flopped(k-1) mod 2³²; add_out_reg(k) = R(k)?0:( M(k) ? a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) : add_out_reg(k-1)+mul_out_reg(k-1) ) mod 2³²; out(k) = R(k)?0:downcast(add_out_reg(k-1)).
  • New sum register: eltsum_reg(k) = R(k)?0:(a(k)+b(k)) = a_flopped(k)+b_flopped(k) on every cycle, reset included, because it takes the same reset at the same edge and a_flopped/b_flopped take the same inputs at that edge. It is 17 bits wide, so it equals the original's {16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped} exactly.
  • Block operand registers: A2(k) = R(k)?0:a_flopped(k-1), B2(k) = R(k)?0:b_flopped(k-1) (CEA2/CEB2 tied high, RSTA/RSTB = reset), so A2(k)·B2(k) = mul_out_reg(k) on every cycle including reset cycles, since the original zeroes the product register exactly when both operand registers are zeroed. Both operands are zero-extended 16-bit unsigned values with a zero top bit on A (27-bit port) and on B (18-bit port), so the signed 27×18 product is exact and non-negative; its low 32 bits are the unsigned 32-bit product.
  • Block P: C and OPMODE are unregistered and act at the edge that loads P, so C at edge k is {31'b0, eltsum_reg(k-1)} = a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) — exactly the eltwise term the original needs at edge k — and the A2·B2 value present at edge k is A2(k-1)·B2(k-1) = mul_out_reg(k-1). Hence P(k) = R(k)?0 (RSTP wins over CEP):( M(k) ? C(k) : P(k-1)+A2(k-1)·B2(k-1) ), and by induction P(k)[31:0] = add_out_reg(k): the 48-bit accumulate taken mod 2³² is the original's 32-bit wrap-around add, and P[47:32] is never read.
  • Resets: reset here is mac_reset = reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) (conv_layer.v:2647). It drives RSTA, RSTB, RSTP and the new eltsum_reg together, reproducing the original's simultaneous clearing of a_flopped/b_flopped, mul_out_reg and add_out_reg on mid-run resets as well as at start-up. The block's registers power up at 0, matching the original's registers starting at 0. OPMODE only ever takes the two listed legal encodings, so P is never loaded under an undefined encoding. When OPMODE is the MAC encoding, C is not used by the block, so an undefined eltsum_reg cannot reach P in MAC mode.

ASSUMES: reset (i.e. mac_reset) is high on at least one clock edge after power-up before the first edge at which eltwise_mode & eltwise_add is high. Only then is the added soft register eltsum_reg defined when C is first used; it is the one place the rewrite depends on a register it adds. The design supports this: mac_reset = reset || (eltwise_mode && (~ready_for_eltwise_op)) (conv_layer.v:2647) and ready_for_eltwise_op = (clk_cnt > final_mat_mul_size) (conv_layer.v:2001) with clk_cnt counting up from 0 out of reset, so every eltwise operation is necessarily preceded by cycles in which eltwise_mode is high while ready_for_eltwise_op is still low, i.e. with mac_reset high; and pe_reset/reset are ORed into every PE at conv_layer.v:2528. The rewrite still matches the original on the reference testbench's stimulus regardless of the assumption, since that testbench holds reset high for its first 4 cycles (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:154-155) and compares only afterwards, by which time eltsum_reg has been cleared to 0; in MAC mode the register's value never reaches P at all.

Blocks added

84 dsp48e2_macc blocks, one per seq_mac instance, on 84 DSP tiles — the same tile count as the unmodified design and as the kept candidate. Soft logic per instance versus the original: removed are 32 product-register flops, 32 accumulator flops, one 32-bit adder and two 32-bit muxes; added are one 17-bit adder, 17 flops and a 1-bit OPMODE mux. Versus the kept candidate the only addition is the 17 flops (1428 across the design).

Register stages, operands to result

  • MAC mode, before: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg, accumulating (3) → out (4).
  • MAC mode, after: a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → block A2/B2 (2) → block P, accumulating (3) → soft out (4). Same four stages, same latency, same accumulation cycle.
  • Eltwise-add mode, before: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → 32-bit soft adder → add_out_reg (2) → out (3).
  • Eltwise-add mode, after: a/b → 17-bit soft adder → eltsum_reg (1) → block C, loaded into P (2) → soft out (3). Same three stages; the adder has simply moved from after stage 1 to before it, and eltsum_reg carries the identical value a_flopped+b_flopped.
Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of seq_mac on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi

// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are

Call 8 · acceptance reviewer · ACCEPT · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 14 · out 7834 · reasoning 5479 · cache read 157989 · cache write 29654 · 101.0 s
prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, the one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" row of UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12 (p46); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p30). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 v1.11 p38: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). AMD's words end with that sentence; the limits that follow are this project's, derived from it. So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. P is always registered; C and OPMODE reach P without an input register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file conv_layer.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

The approved plan

I'll read the source first.

REGIONS: qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339
SHARED_REDUCTION: seq_mac's accumulator (the original add_out_reg / qadd feedback), one per seq_mac instance; no reduction is shared between instances
BLOCKS: 84
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per seq_mac instance (84 instances) — CLK=clk; A={11'b0, a_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, top bit 0); B={2'b0, b_flopped} (16-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, top bit 0); C={31'b0, eltsum_reg} (new 17-bit soft register holding a+b registered one stage earlier, zero-extended to 48); OPMODE=(eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 (load C) : 9'b000100101 (multiply-accumulate); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP=reset (the module's reset, i.e. mac_reset); CEA2=CEB2=CEP=1'b1; output P[31:0] replaces the register add_out_reg and drives the existing down-cast logic that feeds out (P[47:32] unused)
PLAN:

This revision in one line

The kept candidate is correct and already −20.2%; its critical path is now entirely the eltwise-add sum: a 16+16→17-bit soft adder sitting combinationally between a_flopped/b_flopped and the block's unregistered C pin, whose path runs on through the block's 2.140 ns to the P register (measured at seed 1: 0.060 clk-to-Q + 1.939 wire + 0.344 carry chain + 1.001 wire + 2.140 = 5.523 ns arrival). This revision moves that adder one register stage earlier — it computes a + b from the module inputs into a new 17-bit flop eltsum_reg — so C is driven straight from a flip-flop and the C→P stage becomes 0.060 + routing + 2.159 ns, with the adder in its own short flop-to-flop stage. Everything else is the kept candidate unchanged.

Candidate regions

  • qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 (conv_layer.v:2854, o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier, 16×16 unsigned, 84 instances): converted, on 84 blocks. It is the only candidate; nothing is left unconverted. qmult is instantiated only at conv_layer.v:2793 and qadd only at conv_layer.v:2810, both inside seq_mac.

What each block absorbs (unchanged from the kept candidate)

Per seq_mac (conv_layer.v:2765-2846): the qmult product, the product pipeline register mul_out_reg, the accumulator register add_out_reg with its qadd adder and the add_in1/add_in2 muxes. DSP tiles stay at 84 (≤ 306).

Readers re-checked in the source: mul_out is read only by mul_out_reg (2799); mul_out_reg only by add_in2 (2808); add_out only by add_out_reg (2816); add_out_reg by add_in1 (2807) and by the down-cast at 2823-2835; a_flopped/b_flopped by the multiply (2793) and by add_in1/add_in2 (2807-2808); out (2774, 2838-2844) is the module's only output. Nothing inside the block is read elsewhere.

Edits to make, inside module seq_mac only (against the unmodified conv_layer.v in this directory)

  1. Keep the a_flopped/b_flopped always block (2783-2791) exactly as it is; a_flopped/b_flopped now feed only A/B.
  2. Delete wire [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] add_out; (2775), wire [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out; reg [2*\DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_reg; (2780-2781), the qmult mult_u1 instance (2793) and the mul_out_reg` always block (2795-2801).
  3. Delete add_in1, add_in2, the add_out_reg declaration, the two assigns, the qadd add_u1 instance and the add_out_reg always block (2804-2818).

  4. Add the new operand-sum register, reset by the module's reset exactly like a_flopped/b_flopped:

    verilog
      reg [`DWIDTH:0] eltsum_reg;            // = a_flopped + b_flopped, one stage earlier
  always @(posedge clk) begin
    if (reset) eltsum_reg <= 0;
    else       eltsum_reg <= {1'b0, a} + {1'b0, b};
  end

    (17 bits, so the unsigned 16+16 sum never truncates; the explicit {1'b0,…} keeps the width unambiguous.)

  5. Add the control and the block:

    verilog
      wire [8:0]  dsp_opmode = (eltwise_mode & eltwise_add) ? 9'b110000000 : 9'b000100101;
  wire [47:0] P_dsp;
  dsp48e2_macc u_dsp (
    .CLK(clk), .A({11'b0, a_flopped}), .B({2'b0, b_flopped}),
    .C({31'b0, eltsum_reg}), .OPMODE(dsp_opmode),
    .RSTA(reset), .RSTB(reset), .RSTP(reset),
    .CEA2(1'b1), .CEB2(1'b1), .CEP(1'b1), .P(P_dsp));
  wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out_reg;
  assign add_out_reg = P_dsp[2*`DWIDTH-1:0];
  6. Leave the down-cast (2820-2835) and the out always block (2838-2844) untouched; leave the qmult/qadd module definitions in the file (they become uninstantiated and are swept); do not add a body for dsp48e2_macc (the model is supplied in sim_reference/).
  7. Change nothing outside seq_mac.

Why this is cycle-for-cycle identical (edge k samples the values present before it; R(k) = reset at edge k, M(k) = eltwise_mode & eltwise_add at edge k)

  • Original: a_flopped(k) = R(k)?0:a(k), likewise b_flopped; mul_out_reg(k) = R(k)?0:a_flopped(k-1)·b_flopped(k-1) mod 2³²; add_out_reg(k) = R(k)?0:( M(k) ? a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) : add_out_reg(k-1)+mul_out_reg(k-1) ) mod 2³²; out(k) = R(k)?0:downcast(add_out_reg(k-1)).
  • New sum register: eltsum_reg(k) = R(k)?0:(a(k)+b(k)) = a_flopped(k)+b_flopped(k) on every cycle, reset included, because it takes the same reset at the same edge and a_flopped/b_flopped take the same inputs at that edge. It is 17 bits wide, so it equals the original's {16'b0,a_flopped}+{16'b0,b_flopped} exactly.
  • Block operand registers: A2(k) = R(k)?0:a_flopped(k-1), B2(k) = R(k)?0:b_flopped(k-1) (CEA2/CEB2 tied high, RSTA/RSTB = reset), so A2(k)·B2(k) = mul_out_reg(k) on every cycle including reset cycles, since the original zeroes the product register exactly when both operand registers are zeroed. Both operands are zero-extended 16-bit unsigned values with a zero top bit on A (27-bit port) and on B (18-bit port), so the signed 27×18 product is exact and non-negative; its low 32 bits are the unsigned 32-bit product.
  • Block P: C and OPMODE are unregistered and act at the edge that loads P, so C at edge k is {31'b0, eltsum_reg(k-1)} = a_flopped(k-1)+b_flopped(k-1) — exactly the eltwise term the original needs at edge k — and the A2·B2 value present at edge k is A2(k-1)·B2(k-1) = mul_out_reg(k-1). Hence P(k) = R(k)?0 (RSTP wins over CEP):( M(k) ? C(k) : P(k-1)+A2(k-1)·B2(k-1) ), and by induction P(k)[31:0] = add_out_reg(k): the 48-bit accumulate taken mod 2³² is the original's 32-bit wrap-around add, and P[47:32] is never read.
  • Resets: reset here is mac_reset = reset || (eltwise_mode && ~ready_for_eltwise_op) (conv_layer.v:2647). It drives RSTA, RSTB, RSTP and the new eltsum_reg together, reproducing the original's simultaneous clearing of a_flopped/b_flopped, mul_out_reg and add_out_reg on mid-run resets as well as at start-up. The block's registers power up at 0, matching the original's registers starting at 0. OPMODE only ever takes the two listed legal encodings, so P is never loaded under an undefined encoding. When OPMODE is the MAC encoding, C is not used by the block, so an undefined eltsum_reg cannot reach P in MAC mode.

ASSUMES: reset (i.e. mac_reset) is high on at least one clock edge after power-up before the first edge at which eltwise_mode & eltwise_add is high. Only then is the added soft register eltsum_reg defined when C is first used; it is the one place the rewrite depends on a register it adds. The design supports this: mac_reset = reset || (eltwise_mode && (~ready_for_eltwise_op)) (conv_layer.v:2647) and ready_for_eltwise_op = (clk_cnt > final_mat_mul_size) (conv_layer.v:2001) with clk_cnt counting up from 0 out of reset, so every eltwise operation is necessarily preceded by cycles in which eltwise_mode is high while ready_for_eltwise_op is still low, i.e. with mac_reset high; and pe_reset/reset are ORed into every PE at conv_layer.v:2528. The rewrite still matches the original on the reference testbench's stimulus regardless of the assumption, since that testbench holds reset high for its first 4 cycles (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:154-155) and compares only afterwards, by which time eltsum_reg has been cleared to 0; in MAC mode the register's value never reaches P at all.

Blocks added

84 dsp48e2_macc blocks, one per seq_mac instance, on 84 DSP tiles — the same tile count as the unmodified design and as the kept candidate. Soft logic per instance versus the original: removed are 32 product-register flops, 32 accumulator flops, one 32-bit adder and two 32-bit muxes; added are one 17-bit adder, 17 flops and a 1-bit OPMODE mux. Versus the kept candidate the only addition is the 17 flops (1428 across the design).

Register stages, operands to result

  • MAC mode, before: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → mul_out_reg (2) → add_out_reg, accumulating (3) → out (4).
  • MAC mode, after: a/b → soft a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → block A2/B2 (2) → block P, accumulating (3) → soft out (4). Same four stages, same latency, same accumulation cycle.
  • Eltwise-add mode, before: a/b → a_flopped/b_flopped (1) → 32-bit soft adder → add_out_reg (2) → out (3).
  • Eltwise-add mode, after: a/b → 17-bit soft adder → eltsum_reg (1) → block C, loaded into P (2) → soft out (3). Same three stages; the adder has simply moved from after stage 1 to before it, and eltsum_reg carries the identical value a_flopped+b_flopped.

DESIGN_CHANGE: none beyond this rewrite — the previous pass's suggestion (register a + b a stage earlier) is carried out here inside the rewrite itself, since the earlier-registered sum is an exact recomputation of the original value and needs no change to what the design computes. The remaining C→P stage is now the block's own 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 ns plus routing, which cannot be shortened without a block that registers C.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan compliance

  • The only converted region is qmult.$mul$conv_layer.v:2854$339 inside seq_mac; the six listed edits all appear verbatim (conv_layer.v:2776-2806), and the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms nothing outside qadd/qmult/seq_mac changed. qmult/qadd module bodies are left in place (2837-2854) and uninstantiated, as the plan says; no body for dsp48e2_macc was added.
  • Block count: 12 processing_element per systolic_pe_matrix (2570-2587) × 7 matmul_4x4_systolic instances (1309-1549) = 84 seq_mac, one block each → 84 of 306 DSP tiles.

Ports (conv_layer.v:2799-2803): all eleven inputs connected by name — CLK, A={11'b0,a_flopped} (27, MSB 0), B={2'b0,b_flopped} (18, MSB 0), C={31'b0,eltsum_reg} (48), OPMODE, RSTA/RSTB/RSTP=reset, CEA2/CEB2/CEP=1'b1. P drives P_dsp, whose low 32 bits feed only the existing down-cast at 2811-2823, as the plan states; P[47:32] unread.

Behavior

  • A2(k)·B2(k) = mul_out_reg(k) on every cycle including reset cycles (RSTA/RSTB share reset with the original product register); at edge k the block sees A2(k-1)·B2(k-1) = mul_out_reg(k-1), matching the original accumulate.
  • eltsum_reg(k) = a_flopped(k)+b_flopped(k) on every cycle (same inputs, same reset, same edge), 17 bits so no truncation; at edge k, C = that value from k-1 — the term the original eltwise mux needs. 48-bit wrap taken mod 2^32 equals the original's 32-bit wrap; both operands are unsigned 16-bit with a zero top bit, so the signed 27×18 product is exact.
  • OPMODE is unregistered and only ever takes 9'b110000000 or 9'b000100101, both legal; the mux uses eltwise_mode & eltwise_add at the same edge as the original mux. Latency is unchanged in both modes (4 stages MAC, 3 stages eltwise).
  • RSTP wins over CEP, so P(k)=0 exactly when the original zeroed add_out_reg; block registers power up at 0.
  • Visibility: mul_out/mul_out_reg/add_out had no readers outside the absorbed logic (2799, 2808, 2816 in the original); add_out_reg is recomputed outside as P_dsp[31:0] for the down-cast and out.

Assumption carried from the plan and valid as stated: reset (= mac_reset, 2647) is high on at least one edge before the first edge with eltwise_mode & eltwise_add high, so the added soft register eltsum_reg is defined when C is first used; in MAC mode C never reaches P. The simulation reports x_count 0 and 0 mismatches over 1156 cycles with a mid-run reset, and the reset-between-phases diagnostic also passes. (delayed_one 674 is an activity statistic, not a mismatch count.)

output
1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.11 Table 2-12, p46), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -7.26% against first, min-gain 0.037 (baseline seed_figures (area x delay over the entry's seed runs)))
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_small_sdc · used area 6.14515e+07 · critical path 5.68082 ns · wirelength 285909 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 3.49095e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 3.70%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 3.70%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

No schematic pair for this rewrite.

Pass 2 product 2.98642e+08 (-14.45%)
tier
T1
plan header
BLOCKS 84 · TILES · STAGE_NS
assumptions
`reset` (i.e. `mac_reset`) is high on at least one clock edge after power-up before the first edge at which `eltwise_mode & eltwise_add` is high. Only then is the added soft register `eltsum_reg` defined when `C` is first used; it is the one place the rewrite depends on a register it adds. The design supports this: `mac_reset` = `reset || (eltwise_mode && (~ready_for_eltwise_op))` (conv_layer.v:2647) and `ready_for_eltwise_op = (clk_cnt > final_mat_mul_size)` (conv_layer.v:2001) with `clk_cnt` counting up from 0 out of reset, so every eltwise operation is necessarily preceded by cycles in which `eltwise_mode` is high while `ready_for_eltwise_op` is still low, i.e. with `mac_reset` high; and `pe_reset`/`reset` are ORed into every PE at conv_layer.v:2528. The rewrite still matches the original on the reference testbench's stimulus regardless of the assumption, since that testbench holds `reset` high for its first 4 cycles (tb_equiv_seq_mac.v:154-155) and compares only afterwards, by which time `eltsum_reg` has been cleared to 0; in MAC mode the register's value never reaches P at all.
planner call
